So we entered the development of the agenda with point 1 of legislative texts. Comprehensive discussion of the draft law on the financing of political training and related foundations and associations
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u5	This bill has been amended by the full return of the Joint Parliamentary Group, the Group of the Galician Nationalist Block and the Group of Parliamentary Members of the Left Galician Alternative. For the presentation of the bill, the Vice-President and President-in-Office, Public Administrations and Justice, Mr. Alfonso Rueda Valenzuela, has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u6	Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, good morning. Today, the Council of Galicia presents in this House a law that comes to honour a commitment made here by the President of the Council during the debate on the state of autonomy of the year 2014. From this debate you will remember that, among other things, a motion for a resolution that literally urged the Union to promote an agreement for the financing of the restricted parties to the funds that were made in public budgets and to the comings of its affiliates and public offices. We believe, therefore, that this was a matter where we could join together, because there were certain similar pronouncements by various public representatives earlier, beginning with the President of the Board himself, who in January 2013 had already stated in an interview that it was necessary to change the Party Financing Act. Also what at that time was the leader of the opposition at the national level, Mr. Rubalcaba, in February 2013 proposed that companies could not offer prayers to political parties. And a month later – not to mention any more examples – Mr. Cayo Lara, leader of the United Left, defended in Congress the intention of his group to limit prayers to political parties. As a result, this coincidence between different political forces with different opinions on other matters led to this proposal presenting the resolution, which was adopted and which then caused Junta to start working in that direction to comply with it and its content. Thus, in November 2014, a month after that debate on the state of autonomy in which this motion for a resolution was adopted, the Board of Trustees agreed with the Advisory Council on the drafting of a preliminary draft law regulating public subsidies to political parties in the field of autonomy in a single text, which also addressed the scheme of grants to foundations and associations associated with these political parties, and which also dealt with, in so far as they could do so from the scope of autonomous competences, the perception of prayers by legal persons. Thus, after an intense two-month study and report section of the Advisory Council, the full Council gave its visa and enjoyed a text of this preliminary draft at the beginning of February this same year. And I would like to take this opportunity to thank, first of all, the members of the Advisory Council, both of the advisers themselves and of the staff who participated in the preparation of this preliminary draft by the Board of Trustees. I think it was a job that came up with a very complete text, very appropriate from a legal point of view and very technically careful. And so that's why in the administrative process that took place at the Galician Summit there were only changes added, there were no allegations from the councils, there was no repair of any of the legal advice, and only some precisions detected by the gender impact report were introduced. So I've already said that the proceedings were fast and, in less than a month since its elevation as a preliminary draft by the Advisory Council, the text was already forwarded to this House in the form of the bill we are going to discuss today. This is therefore a proposal from the Consultative Council on the moment when the groups approach their proposals, in this parliamentary procedure, with the intention to achieve a text which has the greatest possible consensus, which affects us all; in addition, the political groups will ultimately be the recipients of the forecasts contained in this text. It cannot be obvious that the processing of this bill coincided with the parliamentary process and subsequent approval, in March of the same year, only two months ago, of a reform of the State's basic rules on the financing of parties, with which the text I think is especially appropriate now. It is true that these basic regulations are already gathering some of the requirements that are collected in the current text of the bill, whose drafting has begun before, and even several of the proposals put opposition groups in the formulation of their amendments. But it must also be said that this new basic legislation is no less necessary, as I said, this text, but quite the contrary, I believe that it comes with special opportunity so that in this parliamentary procedure the groups can adapt to this legislation the circumstances of our autonomous community and that they can work on it to introduce new demands, new mechanisms of control, about the political parties in Galicia which are complementary to the provisions of state law, of obligation to comply with, and more appropriate to the circumstances of Galicia. I would also like to highlight some of the new aspects that this text comes up with with regard to state legislation. For example, a scheme of incompatibilities is introduced for the appointment of candidates administrators, who do not exist in the basic law. This bill prevents, for example, people incur legal incompatibility by passive suffrage to occupy these types of positions. It's a proposal from the Advisory Council that we have seen from June and that we ask the groups to take into consideration the novelty of our law. This project also, as a novelty to basic law, introduces mechanisms of greater control in the electoral finances of the Greek political parties by requiring that all funds intended to meet electoral expenses should be entered directly into the bank accounts enabled for this purpose. For what? To make it easier to control the origin of these money that the parties receive, which cannot come from opaque transfers from other accounts but must always enter as direct income on these specifically enabled accounts. It also applies as novelty and for the first time to the Welsh elections a warrant of transparency, which was already in the LOREG but which applied only to the electoral processes when there are general and municipal elections. Well, now it is also obliged that, both financial institutions that grant credits to parties and companies that are hired for election expenses, should also respond to the Board of Auditors when we speak of the elections in Greece, which was not legally binding until now, only – I insist – for municipalities and generals. It is also stated that the electoral grant cannot be abolished until the Board of Auditors certifys that the requirements have been met. It is true that parties are allowed to request advances, but there is a need for guarantees that, if the supervisory body does not finally authorize the grant, the Administration, the Autonomous Community, may recover this money which has been advanced. And it also makes a very specific regulation of the regime of foundations and associations linked to the Greek political parties. In this sense, control by the Board of Auditors of the use these foundations make of subsidies received by the self-employed public sector. This control is also added to what the State Court of Auditors has already done about the donations received by these entities and their annual accounts. And it is also necessary, in a very clear way, that parties cannot receive prayers from their own foundations and associations linked to end all sorts of suspicions about the possibility that the smaller restrictions on foundations with respect to control could not be used to be indirectly financing the party through their own foundation. This will no longer be possible, for not only the controlling agencies must certify that the funds they receive these foundations are used for appropriate purposes, but also the possibility that funds entering through a foundation can be derived from a party to meet expenses of any kind, whatever. I believe, ladies and gentlemen, that these are measures that we can all or should even agree and that it is worth discussing, and it is worth, of course, improving the parliamentary process of this bill. I say that these are complementary measures to those established by the reform of the Basic Law on the financing of parties driven by the Government of the State, measures we all know already; for example, the total prohibition of debt concessions by credit institutions, the total prohibition of donations of legal persons, the limitation of the donations of natural persons to 50,000 euros a year, compared to the 100,000 which have so far been envisaged, and transparency and publication of all prayers greater than 25,000 euros. And so, ladies and gentlemen, I believe that between the installation of own measures that makes this autonomous law and the adaptation that must be made of the measures derived from the basic law, I believe that the processing of this bill is a good opportunity to consolidate a normative corpus on the demand and control of parties' finances as it did not exist until now in Spain or Galicia. No doubt you can go further, of course. You tell me that from the opposition there are proposals for restrictions on other types of donations or requirements for parties to be able to receive them. This is the time to debate them and, if necessary, incorporate them into the text. I believe that these proposals are also worth studying, discussing, and that is why I make this call to try to consensualize, as far as possible, a text which, I insist, will affect all of us from here on. I think this text is in line with the demands of citizenship. I understand that some of the amendments to the whole text are made – or so they say in the amendment – because they are not betted by an eminently public funding system. Well, I have to say that exactly what comes from this bill is precisely an eminently public funding, through these two possible avenues of grant, each with its corresponding monitoring and monitoring mechanism to extreme guarantees. What is derived from this bill and from state policy changes is a significant decrease – that's also true – of the role private donors can have in party activity. I believe that these are the objectives that were intended and the words of the leaders of the political formations I mentioned earlier, and I think that in any of those who could speak on this subject, I insist, of the whole parliamentary arch. I would like to thank the Socialist Group for its position in this respect, which did not, at least, amend altogether. And I believe that this positive willingness to work – or at least so we take it – can facilitate that consensus to which I referred earlier on a text that – I say it again and I say it as often as necessary – will affect everyone. And with regard to the groups that have tabled amendments to the whole, the other groups of this Parliament, I want to think that the presentation is done not from an effective point of view and to seek merely a confrontation between parties in this House but as an initial position, even if it is as a manifestation of intentions, but then trying to reach agreements on amendments once this text enters the normal process, if it exceeds – I hope it will – the vote on amendments to the whole. Of course, our responsibility as legislators, ladies and gentlemen, is to act to provide us with tools that enable us to prevent, solve and react to any issue which, with regard to financing, could be given to parties in the future. That's what this bill is going to be for. That's what you've worked on before the Advisory Council, about which you're going to work now this House. I believe that this text, along with the basic state legislation, should close a set of rules on which there could be no doubt. And from the fact that there are no doubts, there can also be no doubt about the punishment and persecution of anyone who violates such a sensitive issue, about a question that citizenship demands that we be inflexible, that we have clear these things and that we act accordingly. The German Government has done so with this task, and now it does so by presenting this text to this House. Thank you very much.

Good day to all and to all We are faced with a bill that is no more than propaganda that PP uses as a screen to hide reality from ordinary people, though, in view of election results, it seems that this reality is difficult to hide. When at the PP in Galicia, Madrid, Valencia, Castile-A Mancha and throughout the wide and high state, the illegal funding of the party and the electoral campaigns was discovered and refused to assume their political responsibilities, we came with the resounding Bill of Financing of Political Trainings and Related Foundations and Associations. The Anti-Corruption Prosecutor's Office included in his indictment a detailed account of the illegal financing of the PP through a box B hidden from the tax office and the Court of Auditors, which worked for at least years. The Prosecutor maintains that Luis Bárcenas, the PP treasurer, incorporated into his assets resources of the People's Party, whose administration had been entrusted as manager along with the former treasurer Lapuerta, between February 1993 and June 2008. These funds came from a box B that administered Basel along with the party's then treasurer, Alvaro Lapuerta. The funds that supposedly stole Baseles and entered into open accounts in the State and Switzerland came from the parallel accounting of the People's Party. This parallel accounting – according to the prosecutor – would be nourished by donations that would violate the rules of financing political parties and that were made by persons related to entities benefiting from important public adjudications. But the thing remains not only in the Bárcenas and Lapuerta, but it has already come from Naseiro, and, if it had been little, it is not the only reason open for illegal corruption and financing that the People's Party has; even the press has to use paintings and diagrams to be able to follow up the cases of corruption and illegal financing of the People's Party, speaking, for example, of a payment in black money in Pontevedra that caused the embargo of a People's Party account, among many other corruptions. The Anti-Corruption Prosecutor claims 125 years of imprisonment and fine of 15.5 million to Correa, the leader of Gürtel, of the plot of corruption that colonized the People's Party from the end of the 1990s until February 2009, when Judge Baltasar Garzón finished his business. He also asks for forty-two and a half years for the Bárcenas and sentences of deprivation of liberty for the 41 processed in the first phase of the investigation. These include public offices of the People's Party, such as the former vice-counsel of the Presidency of the Madrid Community, Alberto López Viejo, former mayors of Pozuelo de Alarcon and Majadahonda or former national treasurers of the PP, such as the Bárcenas, Lapuerta or Angel Sanchis themselves, who have managed the party's finances for more than 20 years. Judge Pablo Ruz believed that the People's Party had a box B between 1990 and 2008 with which he had electoral campaigns, superseded to leaders and local reforms, including that of the headquarters in Madrid. The judge considers that there are solid and well-founded indications that the PP served during those eighteen years, systematically, from various sources of funding other than the legal economic circuit. Despite the resistance of the People's Party and the Tax Agency, the judge concludes that the opaque funds of the PP flowed to the edge of the accounting sent to the Court of Auditors and without the corresponding statement to the public estate; the People's Party's resistance to the destruction of the hard disks of the computers of Basel, guarded by the PP and on which there is an investigation opened by Judge Ruz. As for the Tax Agency, let's remember that you've come to claim that donations that exceed the legal limit are exempt from taxation, just like legal ones. In addition, the Tax Agency came to compare the People's Party with Caritas, to justify the tax exemption from illegal funds. Ruz rejects the popular party's thesis of assigning the system of black donations to Bárcenas, but rather to the People's Party itself. Throughout the car, expressions such as "the double accounting structure created by the People's Party", "a system of financing political formation" or "funds of the People's Party's Box B" abound. The Bárcenas and his predecessor Laportta the judge punishes them from the parallel accounting of the People's Party. In the entry section, details the contributions of the entrepreneurs, among those who stand out names such as Juan Miguel Millar Mir or Luis del Rivero, of Sacyr. According to Judge Ruz's auto, part of the black money entered – split, not to exceed the limits of the Party Financing Act – in the official donor accounts – 1.27 million entered –. Another party was destined to pay salaries and surpluses of people linked to the party. Other amounts have been used to help victims of terrorism. There are also expenses for election campaigns and for buying premises like Biscay's or Rioxa's. And, as it does not, Ruz highlights the EUR 1.55 million in B between 2005 and 2010 to reform the headquarters of the PP in Madrid. In his car, Judge Ruz considers the PP to be responsible for a tax offence for not paying the illegal donations received in 2008, and opens a [...] separate from the Toledo garbage collection contract, which, according to Bárcens, was the counterbalance for a €200,000 donation for the campaign of the President of Castile-A Mancha, Maria Dolores de Cospedal, in 2007. Ruz appreciates a crime of prevarication in this case. In addition, this bill does nothing but constantly refer to Organic Law 5/1975, of June 19, of general electoral regime, and regulates very often subsidies to the functioning of political parties in Galicia which neither existed nor existed. The problem of financing parties is the handling of money in black as a counterparty of favors in public concessions, and this is only solved by giving justice the means that PP denies it and broadens the deadlines for the prescription of crimes so that they do not continue to go out indemnity to the treasury of the People's Party, as has been the case since the time of Naseiro. The Anti-Corruption Prosecutor and the Tax Agency, in relation to the Gürtel case, issued reports that attributed to the People's Party of Madrid an offence of illegal financing through Fundescan, an opaque foundation of the party that raised hundreds of thousands of euros among businessmen close to the right, such as Gerardo Díaz Ferrán, Arturo Fernández or Antonio Basagoiti, who later paid bills for the 2003 and 2004 campaigns of Esperanza Aguirre. But one cannot act against those responsible, because crimes for alleged illegal financing, manipulation of documents, even a possible rocket, so the generous donors of Fundescan subsequently signed millions of contracts with the Government of Aguirre, have already prescribed. So the important thing is the legal changes that prevent these crimes from end up prescribing and, therefore, they end up imposing those who committed them. The bill runs away from taking on real responsibilities. Beyond this bill, the Government should demand and agree with the State the transfer of powers to general electoral systems, and make use of Article 152 of the Spanish Constitution, which states that the State may transfer or delegate to autonomous communities by means of organic law powers corresponding to matters of State ownership, which, by their very nature, are susceptible to transfer or delegation. Also make use of Article 10(f) of the Statute of Self-Government, to request the Government to adopt a bill on the subject when presenting a bill before the Bureau of the Congress of Members. Similarly, it could address the reform of prescriptions and penalties for crimes related to corruption; crimes that are not sufficiently punished, crimes that have a great imbalance with other penalties, common crimes of theft, often in order to survive, that are severely punished and that, in addition to being added – we talked about the other day of the prison competitions – they end up becoming permanent prison sentences, while the great thieves, the big corrupt ones, those who steal "white gloves", usually overcome outside the prison, or their penalties ended up being much lower for various aid provided to them from the State itself. For all the above and because we're not going to allow this law to be used to do a clean-up of the People's Party's face with regard to corruption, we're going to vote against this bill. We will be ready to pass a bill on the financing of political parties in Galicia when the People's Party is prepared to also assume election powers and claim powers to be able to govern, but above all we will be prepared to talk about political parties' funding when the People's Party clears its ranks of corruption and pays what [...] to all the citizens of the state; when they clear their accounts. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u10	Thank you, Madam President. Mr Vice-President, ladies and gentlemen, with this bill we have seen another chapter of the lift operation undertaken by the Government of Mr Feijóo, a lift operation which, in addition to this particular case, recalls the joke of Gila, I don't know if you know it: a person wanted to do an aesthetic surgery to take out twenty years, but, as the budget was not enough for him, he had to comply with taking a week. In this case we are the same, because first the President of the Board proposed in this Parliament a pact to ban company grants from political formations, and then the Council approved the so-called "Democratic impulse plan", which expressly collected, in its 8th axis, deleting anonymous prayers or legal persons in the financing of parties. After these far-fetched pledges, how do we find ourselves? By which in this bill no concessions are prohibited, the non-perception of concessions is simply established as a condition for parties to be able to access grants to their ordinary functioning from the budgets of the Union; grants to ordinary functioning, of course, that do not exist, and in which in addition to this project they do not even consider themselves to be created imperatively. Finally, Mr. Feijó missed his word again. In contrast to the announcer, no clear and severe restrictions are prohibited, no direct measures of public compulsion prevent these private incomes or mechanisms for clearing responsibilities and imposing sanctions on the non-compliant party. Failure to accept concessions depends only on the party's will, not on a coercive regulation applicable in any case or situation. In short, a new smoke curtain, a new distraction maneuver, removing empty proposals from the joke, lacking real content, to cover the lack of real, practical commitment from the People's Party, in fighting corruption. Look, you gentlemen of the Government of Galicia and the People's Party, will probably argue that this debate is over, since a state law of 30 March this same year, a law to which you have alluded, the Organic Law on the Control of the Economic and Financial Activity of Political Parties, has already suppressed the donations of legal persons to the parties. But the fact is that you in this bill do not contemplate the abolition of prayers. And besides, as the saying goes, whoever does the law cheats, because the new state law says there can be no prayers of legal persons to the parties, but to their foundations. The new law says that there can be no company grants to parties, but that it can talk about their directors or their shareholders. You can't just ACS or OHL, but Florentino Pérez and Villar Mir. The new law limits the donations of natural persons to a maximum of €50,000, but donations of immovable property which can then be immediately resold are excluded from this limit. That's why he said who makes the law makes the trap. But besides that, Mr. Vice-President and the People's Party gentlemen, it is not worth reforming the law if it does not succeed. Because, look, the B offerings were already illegal, the offerings of companies that hire the administrations were already illegal, and the papers of Basel and the investigations of the Gürtel plot reveal that the Galician People's Party was a central part in the alleged illegal financing plot of the People's Party at the state level. But it is more, now we know, following the revelations of Rosendo Naseiro's documents – the first treasurer of the People's Party – that the People's Party has been illegally financed since its foundation and that the People's Party in Galicia has remained a central part. Look, according to this information, even Manuel Fraga personally received illegal donations to fund the People's Party. According to this information – they went out in the press with Mr Naseiro's documents accompanying this information – Mr. Manuel Fraga won the 1989 elections, in which he arrived at the Presidency of the Union, making traps. The legal limit for campaign spending was 235 million pesetas. The People's Party declared expenditure for 199 million, but Mr Naseiro's papers show that the Galician People's Party received 500 million of the old Spanish People's Party pesetas to fund the campaign, and everything indicates that most of them were from illegal donations. So instead of lifting operations, the first thing you have to do is comply with the law and assume your responsibilities for having consistently failed it. But, look, going beyond these issues, the Galego Nationalist Block is in favour of the public funding of political parties – of course, with the complementarity of quotas, the contributions of militants and supporters –. As much as in the current times it may be unpopular in certain sectors to defend a model of financing that is mainly supported by public funding, we claim that democracy is expensive, but it is much more expensive to the absence of democracy. We don't cultivate anti-political and anti-party populism, history has taught us so much that it leads. We are therefore in favour of a model of public funding for parties, first of all, because it is the model that ensures transparency, and also because it is what ensures the independence of political training. Look, Mr Vice-President, in the extraordinary monographic plenary held last January, in our intervention we talk about the existence of a systemic corruption that has to do with plutocracy, by making the belt of transmission of the interests of economic elites, and we talk about the great corruption as the mechanism used by the economic power to influence public decisions for their benefit. For the dependence on private financing creates the broth of cultivation so that the policy of rotational doors will camp to its widths. Dependence on private financing favours parties that end up acting as simple belts for transmission of the interests of the large economic groups, of the large pressure groups. But it's possible to bet on a public financing model of the parties without this entailing more spending. And in this project, on the other hand, no measures are envisaged to regulate electoral activities more rationally, which absorb an important part of the expenditure of political training. It can considerably reduce the expenditure – by setting an example – by increasing the coverage of election campaigns in the public media, by establishing, for example, the obligation to hold and issue debates and that the broadcast of these debates is at times of maximum audience and also by establishing the obligation that public media broadcast interviews with candidates and candidates from different political training times. In this way, a stricter limitation of campaign expenses can be established, preventing parties from having to resort to over-advertising and advertising to disseminate their messages. But, moreover, that public media become the main broadcasters of debates and messages of all political formations would allow citizens who had access to a greater contrast to the various proposals and therefore a more direct knowledge of the different proposals. It can also reduce spending – by setting another example – by taxing the distribution of ballots and electoral envelopes by political parties and sending electoral propaganda by mail. The Board's own reports reveal that it's the main expenditure chapter. Simply – to cite another example – BNG's decision not to mail in the electoral campaign that has just been held meant saving the state half a million euros. But in addition to a saving measure, it's a democratic hygiene measure. The ban on mailing and distributing envelopes and paperwork by the parties and establishing the obligation to use the electoral booth would ensure the secret of vote and prevent tactics of control and pressure on the voter who continue to be on the agenda in many municipalities in our country. This bill also does not include exemplifying measures to punish captors, such as measures proposed by the BNG in its partial amendments, as it also proposes the savings measures I have just cited. What measures do we propose as an exemplar in order to punish the defaulters? That political formations should believe they are aware of the payments to the Fair and Social Security in order to access the grants. And, above all, that the payment of grants is suspended when judgements – even if not firm – are given where the criminal responsibility of political training or persons with responsibility in the management of their finances is declared, or when in the course of a judicial investigation precautionary measures are agreed against political training and those responsible for it. None of this, Mr Vice-President, is envisaged in this project; it is only a reflection of the pre-existing legislation. It's an adaptation to some precepts, even a copy, of state regulation. It contains no relevant novelty to advance in transparency that should be demanded of any political party operating in Galicia. We are faced with an erratic initiative in the approach and little ambitious, only addresses more profusely, but without introducing important news, the widespread economic activity in the electoral campaigns. Finally, Mr Vice-President, ladies and gentlemen, the German Government, with its President at the front, is again advertising a matter as sensitive as that of democratic regeneration to cover the people's lack of commitment and real action on this issue. This project does not address the substantive problems and, in addition, the Government of Greece, with its President in front of it, lacks the commitments made before this Parliament itself. These are the reasons why we present this amendment to the whole. Thank you very much.

And that's what the People's Party is doing. Since 2012 a law on the financing of political parties in Spain has been passed, two reforms of the Criminal Code have been carried out, and in Galicia we are also now considering reforming the Law on the financing of parties in the field of the Autonomous Community. Because party financing is certainly an important issue and is also connected with everything else. It is certainly connected with the possibility of opening paths to corruption. But it is also connected with citizen participation, because indeed, the quotas of militancy, the free contributions made by citizens who support certain political options establish a link between the party's leadership, which, if all funding was public because it would be much weaker; therefore, it also affects citizen participation and political parties themselves. It is obviously also related to the salaries of the so-called class of persons who hold public office and, of course, is also related to the capacity and relations of political forces, political parties, the rest of the State institutions, and in particular this relationship is very clear in the Court of Auditors or the Council of Auditors. And it's also related to electoral competition. Not as you usually say. There's an interesting study by economists Steven Levitt and Stephen Harper, who actually do an analysis of the thousand electoral confrontations that the same candidates had to be elected to the courts, in the equivalent, in the United States Congress. And they come to the conclusion that there are actually better results when there's more spending, but that's not a causal-effect relationship, and they demonstrate it by the fact that a candidate who increases 50% of the electoral expenditure only averages 1% of these 1,000 confrontations, only gets 1% of the average votes, and those who decreases 50% just fall their vote by 1%. So what's usually happening is that candidates who have options to win are funded and candidates who have no options to win are not funded, and on the other hand, it's completely logical and easy to understand. For all this, and under the premise that political parties are essential in our representative democracy, but that they are private organizations that generate public goods – ideas about good society and therefore proposals to improve the life of citizenship – that is why they require to finance their activities. And so it establishes this – this central role – article 6 of the Constitution when it points out that they are the expression of pluralism, essential for the conformation of the popular will and channels of citizen participation in politics. Well, being everything, no law – you have to start by recognizing it – is free of inconveniences. Normally, political parties can be financed by three legal channels and by one that is not legal: private donations, militant quotas and public subsidies. The fourth is what we try to avoid: the donations that are made aside from the channels established by the rules. The quotas of the parties know that, on average, in Europe, at most, they bring up a quarter of the resources of political training, and from there the debate is whether funding should be eminently private, there should be public funding or, on the other hand, should go to mixed funding. Private funding alone has advantages: the involvement with society and of course the freedom that citizens have to support the ideas they believe in, but it has disadvantages: the contributing inequality of corporations is not the same as that of a citizen of the common and also the difficulty in controlling the donors of companies, when they, in addition, make up holdings and part of a holding company contracts with public administrations and others do not. This system, therefore, is based and operates in the United States and partly in the United Kingdom, in an Anglo-Saxon culture which, in addition, has another way of understanding what the underprivileged are and which certainly is translated not only in the field of political medicates, but also in all kinds of medicates, with a tax system that welcomes them and with a society that sees them well, even though the prayer is for the party that one does not support. The second option is direct grants. 70 per cent of resources in continental Europe come from this type of subsidies, and are based on the necessary independence of the parties, as well as on the nature of the public welfare of political training. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u45	Thank you, President. Good morning, gentlemen. I think it is necessary to initiate this intervention by reminding them that the previous 1983 Act, of 18 April, on the protection of domestic animals and wild animals in captivity, was modified a little less than a year ago by Law 8/2014 of 26 September, and also to remind them that, despite some of them, Galicia is a part of the Spanish State, and it is evident that as part of that Spanish State and as part of this set of State-wide traditions also affect our autonomous community. Any practice during childhood, during management, during the slaughter of any animal produces a certain level of suffering to a greater or lesser extent. And I think it was demonstrated here by this amendment of the law that by all groups there was a sensitivity to avoid such animal abuse. The truth is, whoever is free from sin, who takes away the first stone. And I believe that no one escapes who governs in the Pontevedra Council, no one escapes that Mr. Paco Vázquez was a promoter of certain Taurine shows in the Coruña during his government stage and also funding certain Taurine activities through coalition governments of the Galician Nationalist Block, because we understand that he also had a certain responsibility on the maintenance and promotion of these kinds of shows and such activities. But we went on to report the changes I made to this law: Amendments which were essentially aimed at analysing Article 24 thereof, changing the penalties and the typification of the infringements referred to in Article 24, specifying the slight ones with a content of between EUR 100 and 500, the penalties of EUR 501 to 5,000 and the very serious ones of EUR 5001 to EUR 30,000. The additional third provision of the Act on the Protection of Domestic and Wild Animals in captivity is amended as follows: "The Galician Union shall be authorized to amend the amount of sanctions set out in Article 24 annually according to the annual change in the CPI". An additional provision is added, which is drafted as follows: "The Government must develop together with the protective entities and collaborative associations campaigns and awareness-raising initiatives on the content of this law by the general population." And it also establishes an additional fifth provision, which is defined as: "The revenue from the sanctions imposed by the competent bodies of the General Administration of the Autonomous Community of Galicia for infringements of this law shall be devoted to actions aimed at promoting animal protection." It is clear that these changes have taken one more step and it is clear that by the Galician Union they are already making dissemination campaigns to raise awareness not only of the adult population, but also of the child population, to try to avoid as far as possible any kind of animal abuse practice. But you here today are putting forward a proposal that focuses on modifying it again, almost in less than a year, in Law 1/1993, to remove paragraphs 2 and 3 of Article 5 of that law. These articles say: "The party of bulls, locks and other Taurine shows are excluded from the scope of this law." I also wanted someone to think if the Boi Party in Allariz to consider a show, to consider a tradition, to consider a closure or what kind of consideration they give to that kind of show, to set an example. And it also states at the third point: "Without prejudice to paragraph 1, the Agriculture, Gandery and Mountains Council may authorize, under the control of its respective federation, the holding of firefighting to the Pombino." The autonomic legislation on the protection of domestic animals and wild animals in captivity is basically represented by the law we used to refer to, and also by Decree 153/1998 of 2 April, which develops it. And over 20 years after the publication of the main standard for the protection of domestic animals in Galicia, you can say that it generally collects all those most important points in the fight against abuse or abandonment of domestic animals. On the one hand, it identifies in great detail aspects relating to inadequate conditions regarding the nature of domestic animals; it includes such important aspects as hygienic conditions, sanitation, feeding or the purposes for which animals are used and irregular sale itself; it also identifies ill-treatment in all its variants, including both those situations in which pain is caused and where it does not occur; it may be psychological abuse or on its own conditions in maintenance; it also clearly regulates the obligations of the municipalities on the collection and care of abandoned domestic animals; it recognises the figure of protected animal associations; it establishes the obligation to identify all dogs without exception; and establishes clear criteria for the creation of establishments that house domestic animals and wild animals in captivity. The fact is, at the state level, most of the autonomous standards maintain a great similarity in their structure, in their contents, in the measures envisaged and, above all, especially in the identification of prohibited conduct, if they are well graduated differently from the time of the imposition of sanctions. At Community level, we also find great diversity in the content of animal protection rules and also in the results obtained from their application. All of this resulted in the adoption on 27 June 2012 of a resolution on the protection of domestic animals, in which, among other measures, the European Commission was asked to propose a European Union legal framework for the protection of the welfare of pet animals and vagrant animals. The 1993 Act itself was recently amended, establishing an update on sanctions, not only in the planning of actions to be developed but also in the proactive exercise of awareness-raising and education, through the drive of cooperation through social awareness campaigns that deepen this problem and enable progress towards reducing cases of animal abuse, good practices and responsible tendency. It is also true that the Greek Government is aware of the need to develop awareness-raising initiatives on animal protection. And that's why he's launching campaigns and outreach and education activities aimed primarily at the smaller ones, betting on the values related to animal protection in future generations. And it is also true that the main strategy by which the Council of Galicia is committed, and given that in this community the Taurine shows celebrated is a residual quota and that every year it experiences a declining trend, and by these aspects we understand that it is not considered appropriate to address in the short term and unilaterally a timely modification of the law in terms formulated, but that it is fundamental, and as it cannot be otherwise, to push forward the feeling and actual participation of citizens on this subject, not based solely on uncontrastded and exclusively on data, and exclusively on the basis of the data of opinion. In any case, any revision of these aspects should be part of a framework, in the more comprehensive revision of this law, which guarantees information and public participation processes on an objective basis. Therefore, we understand that in these circumstances it is not necessary to change these two points because the law itself collects perfectly that the use of animals in shows, fights, popular parties and other activities if this can cause harm, suffering or make them subject to unnatural treatment. Exceptionally, the Agriculture, Gandery and Mountains Council may authorize customary shows involving animals. So this change in those two points, we understand that in those circumstances it is not justified.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u55	Good morning. Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I come again in this House to take account of the annual employment policy plan of our community, which will constitute our roadmap for this year 2015 in the field of employment. I compare, as I said, once again – it's the second time – to speak of the most important, of what we are most concerned and occupied, that is employment, and especially because it's our main objective. I'm not speaking in any way of an objective that is new, since, when we arrived at the Government in 2009, we had it very clear, that it was our main task, our irrevocable task, to improve these unemployment figures that accompany us. So from day one, and from minute one, we had very clear that this government's first goal had to be to stop, first of all, the rate of unemployment destruction that was in our community, to then start creating jobs, and today I can say that we are on that path. In the previous legislation we were able to slow down the rate of unemployment growth, in the legislation of 2009, especially since when we arrived there were 50,000 more unemployed in the last year of the previous Government, and in this legislation, in the legislation we are in, we are already moving forward in job creation. And I don't say it, it's not a statement that's simply put forward by a government, but that's what the figures say, the indicators say, that show that today we're not only better than a year ago, but also that we're better than two years ago, three years and even four years ago. So in 2009, the unemployment rate of the labour force survey rose by 3.75 compared to the previous year and now not only not only increases, but is falling by 1.3%. Four years ago, membership of Social Security was decreasing at an annual rate of 2.23% and today membership of Higher Social Security is growing at 2.2%. When we arrived, membership of Social Security lost 45,000 workers and today, in the last year, earns 20,000 members of Social Security. But, if you can imagine, maybe the data that best shows change is the trend towards those 50,000 unemployed unemployed who have been in the last year, between 2008 and 2009, and today there are 25,000 unemployed less. That's why we've been down 18 months consecutively in the inter-annual unemployment rate, that is, 18 months in which unemployment decreases over the previous year. And it's been 13 consecutive months with positive figures also in the membership of Social Security. Finally, they are changing these negative indicators that accompanied us, to become indicators that are on the path of growth, indicators that are positive. Anyway, of course, we have unemployment figures in our community -- we're talking about 244,700 unemployed -- which make us work the whole Government of Galicia every day to change the situation of those people who are unemployed. Of course, economic recovery and job creation are our objective and, of course, job creation must be a quality job. The recruitment data in our community also begin to show positive signs. In December 2011, indefinite contracts were reduced at a rate of 37% annually and this year undefined contracts increase at an annual rate of 12%. And another fact also to be taken into account by analyzing the type of contracts, according to the survey of active population, the percentage of employees in the first quarter of 2007 with indefinite contract, the percentage of employees with indefinite contract in 2007, in the first quarter of 2007, was 68.9%, and now, in the first quarter of 2015, the percentage of undefined contracts within those who show the active population survey is 76.4%, i.e. 7.5 points more than before the crisis, in particular in the first quarter of 2007. On the other hand, population survey data also show that now in Galicia, eight out of ten workers currently have a full-time day. In fact, full-time workers in our community amount to 838,000, out of the 987,500 people occupied by the active population survey. But, of course, we have to keep working, working to improve, working to improve the economic situation and also working for the unemployed in our community. And that is why I would like to focus on this appearance at the Ministry of Labour, which is the annual employment policy plan. As you know, all autonomous communities present to the state the measures that we are going to develop in our community during this year. At this meeting, which has already taken place in April and of what I want to report in this Parliament, the results of the previous plan have also been evaluated, and that is why the new allocation of active employment policy funds for the communities has been made. Thanks to the good implementation of these funds and the fulfilment of the objectives set for 2014, Galicia will receive, in this year 2015, nearly 114 million euros for the concept of active employment policies. This means 14% more than last year. And, as I say, this is because of the good execution of the previous program, that Galicia has increased this percentage that is being dealt with from the central government. In addition, in the presentation of the Annual Employment Action Plan we have shown that we are an active community, the most active one, presenting programmes and presenting measures. Thus, out of the 442 programs that were presented by all autonomous communities, 88 correspond to this autonomous community. That is, 20% of the total annual employment plan across the state was provided by this autonomous community. So we have the community that most programs have included, with these 88 measures, of which 45 are programs that will be developed only in our autonomous community and not in any other. One in five employment programmes in autonomous communities in 2015 will therefore be developed in Galicia. These 88 programmes and measures will have six axes of action. The first of these measures is designed for guidance, because it is essential that workers get a job according to their abilities. The second will be designed for training, to improve the training of our workers and also to improve their opportunities to find a job. The third includes measures aimed at employment opportunities. The fourth is an aspect that we must not forget, which is equal opportunities for access to employment, with the objective that opportunities be the same, regardless of the territory in which we are in. The fifth is the area of action for a collective that we have been supporting from the very first moment, which is the collective of entrepreneurs, through entrepreneurship measures that are, of course, made with all the opportunities and facilities possible to develop their project. And the last – but not least – axis is designed to improve the institutional framework of the national employment system, because undoubtedly improving coordination between administrations is also improving the facilities and opportunities of our workers. Thus, in total, Galicia this year will have a total of 164 million euros for employment, i.e. 12 million more than we anticipated in drawing up our budgets presented to this Parliament for this year. This increase is possible because Galicia will receive 14% more state funds this year, as I said, thanks to having met the objectives and implementation of the previous funds. Within the annual employment policy plan, I would like to focus on those most important measures and, where appropriate, more innovative. Training becomes a fundamental cornerstone of our policies. The whole training is going to be devoted in particular to EUR 160 million, EUR 70 million, a figure that is finally above the estimate that we made in our budget of EUR 66 million. And this is possible because in the distribution of funds, as I said earlier, Galicia obtained the maximum budget and more budget than the previous year. With them we will offer training to more than 15,000 pupils and students. Among these are two important developments. This data converts Galicia into the first community with a higher reduction in unemployment among children under 25 years of age in 2014. In addition, and in addition to the Youth Employment Plan, this year we launched the Youth Guarantee Programme, which is also a novelty in the Annual Employment Policy Plan, which for the first time includes measures specifically designed for children under the age of 25 who do not study or work. It is, as you know, a European programme in which all development measures with social agents have been agreed in our community. We all work with the aim of ensuring that these young people receive an offer of work, of continuing education, of training for employment, or the possibility of practical practice, and for that we are working with them within a maximum of four months since they finish formal education or get unemployed. In other words, in one year we have set up two completely complementary employment plans designed to improve the employability of our young people, and we do so by mobilising 150 million euros for that. On the other hand, we have also designed a specific programme for another of the most concerned groups, who are the long-term unemployed and who also have family burdens; a programme whose measures also appear for the first time in the Annual Employment Policy Plan; that is, for the first time this plan includes measures aimed at people who have been unemployed for more than a year without finding employment; a programme that we have also developed and agreed with social agents and that we have allocated 10 million euros, and which is complementary to the accompanying income of 426 euros that I have put in place the Central Government along with social agents. Galicia is thus the first community in the entire state to design a complementary plan for this collective. Ramón Vázquez Díaz, on the actions to be carried out by the Government of Greece in relation to the application in Galicia of the Organic Law for the improvement of educational quality
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u146	This motion has been tabled amendments by the Socialist Parliamentary Group and the Parliamentary Group of the Nationalist Block Galego.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u147	Mr Vázquez has the floor for the defence of the motion.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u148	Good afternoon, everyone. Today we live one day that in a way is the foreclosure of a day that can be considered harmful for education in our educational system. If no one prevents it, tomorrow it will try to impose more than 22,000 pupils aged 7 and 8 in Galicia on the application of the revalid calls, those already known as LOMCE revalids. Revalids that are trying to impose in an absolutely hasty manner and even irregularly, bordering on the failure to comply with the prevailing legality. The cited reviews do not appear in any of the operating and organizing documents of the centres; they do not appear in the curriculum projects of the stage; they do not appear in the general curriculums of the classrooms and do not even appear in the general evaluation plans that have to be carried out and prepared in the centres themselves. They don't record, it can't be what they consist, because the primary decrees were published in the CEO on 9 September, just one day before he started school. It is therefore a more evident demonstration of the precipitation with which the Government of Greece is trying to put in place a law which is rejected by the entire educational community. But we're not only in the face of a precipitated test, full of opacity, but also in the face of a proof that it's clearly anti-pedagogic, a test that is standardizing, classifying and fundamentally discriminatory to all the student in Greek. It's anti-pedagogic because it doesn't incorporate any kind of improvement on current evaluation processes, and always subject to advanced criteria. Currently, with the legislative framework in force in Galicia, every 3rd primary school student is subject to an initial evaluation within the first 15 days of the course to determine the starting point of this student in dealing with the teaching and learning processes. At this moment, with this normative framework, primary 3 students are subject to an ongoing evaluation, throughout the school course, which involves daily school records by the teacher and the development, the start-up, of interviews, whenever necessary, with the parents and mothers of the children. At this time, a 3rd grade child of primary education undergoes at least 3 evaluation sessions throughout the course, with his corresponding evaluation reports sent to the families, which include both quantitative and qualitative observations of his progress in teaching and learning processes. In addition, those who are known as notebooks are forwarded to all families. At this point, a 7- or 8-year-old is given a final cycle report, which is also referred to as families, which contains a series of information, let's say deep about their ability to move forward in teaching and learning processes. Therefore, families are receiving complete information, complete information from both the process and the outcome of the teaching and learning processes. Article 2 of the order governing primary evaluation states that evaluation, in our educational system, has to be continuous, has to be global, has to be formative and has to be integrative. That's what our normative framework says, and that's all the opposite of what the LOMCE revals suppose. Therefore, evaluation has that formative character, it has that integrative character, because it aims primarily at three questions: retraining the teaching and learning processes at the time when there is some kind of difficulty in the student; establishing compensating measures, precisely to address any kind of difficulty there may be; and, of course, a third, most important purpose, giving the appropriate information, transferring that information to the families of the children and the children evaluated. Therefore, the model that modern pedagogy contemplates for evaluation is an evaluation focused both on processes and results, and therefore not exclusively finalist. And then everything was evaluated. In the evaluation of minimally modern education systems, not only does the student evaluate, the teacher evaluates the school, ultimately evaluates the adjustment, the relevant adjustment of the education system to the needs of the student. We are therefore not in a model of evaluation focused solely on the achievement of these so-called competences by the student. The revalids, therefore, do not improve the existing evaluation system, they do not incorporate any improvements; but they do not intend to do so either, the revalids are not conceived to improve our evaluation model. What they want, their clearly defined function, is to classify and carry out rankings of centers to enhance precisely the choice of centres with better results and, therefore, the privatization of the education system. Say it to LOMCE itself. Article 89 is very clear in this regard and literally says, "Tests will be standardized and designed in such a way that they can establish fair valuations and comparisons. Equitable valuations and comparisons. Article 90 clearly states: The results of the evaluations will be placed in community knowledge through common indicators – we speak of standardization, we do not do it free of charge for all Spanish teaching centres. So this is the key. The revalids are designed, precisely, to measure, to compare, to classify. He recognizes the LOMCE itself. So our disagreement with the revalids is not based only on epistemological and scientific reasons; our disagreement with the revalids is not based only on ideological reasons, but also on our disagreement emanating from the deeply discriminatory and unfair nature of these tests. Tests cannot be established with decontextualized items of the reality object of study, in this case of the socio-educational reality of Galicia. A poor Trives educational center cannot be compared to an educational center in Vallecas. You can't compare the school in a neighborhood of Vigo to a concerted school in downtown Ourense. You cannot compare such different realities by applying identical items. It's a complete aspiration... which is an aberration in educational practice, in pedagogical practice. So what is going to be offered with the results of these revamps is a reality, a distorted image, to nothing true to what the performance of students is. An absolutely distorted educational reality of our education system. And we don't just say it ourselves, the ANPA confederations are reporting these days, and they've already done it before, that truly discriminatory character of the revals. The confederations themselves of parent and mother associations demand that these tests be paralyzed, and they have even sent information to parents and mothers so that children are not taken to school during these tests. So there are many families that have decided not to subject to this unnecessary stress; all evaluation, all measurement incorporates a stress component for the subject evaluated or examined. And, therefore, they themselves defend the right of their children not to be subjected to a test that will not be incorporated, that will not have an academic character, as even the Councillory, the Council of Galicia, recognizes. So the families won't accept the blackmail they're subjected to by the German Government. The Education Council, the Galician board, called the chapter to the directors of the centres, at a provincial meeting, where the directors of the centres were instructed not to transfer information to the families about the voluntary nature of the tests and simply...
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u149	Go finish, please.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u150	...to transfer official information. But there's something even more serious. The Education Council sent a letter to families full of falsehoods and lies, providing entirely distorted information about what evidence is, even in serious lies. There are municipalities, like the council of Ourense, that have already enabled mechanisms so that those families who decide not to send their children to school have spaces and places to be able to reconcile family and work life. But it's not necessary not to send children to school. Any family who comes up with a letter with their son in which he says he does not want his son to submit to this absolutely discriminatory and anti-pedagogic test has the right not to be subjected to that test, since he does not appear in any official document. So what constitutes the motion we are presenting today is very clear: they paralyze the revals planned for tomorrow and immediately call a bilateral commission with the Government to study ways of normative application of LOMCE that do not harm the identity of the Greek people, the Greek culture, the sociodemographic reality of Galicia and, of course, that do not waste the pedagogical advances achieved for decades and that this law, of course, if no one prevents it, will take it forward. Nothing else. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u151	Thank you, Mr. Vázquez. Parliamentary Group of the Nationalist Block Galego. Mr. Pombo.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u152	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, good afternoon. The BNG Parliamentary Group has always advocated delaying the application of LOMCE in Galicia, both in primary and secondary, to avoid the negative consequences it is introducing in the Greek education system. The Wind Business Association considers that energy reform was the cause of parón, the legal insecurity it generates, and the adoption of a new pay system that allows to modify economic conditions every six years without knowing the methodology to be used. According to the calculation of the wind business association, the average hours of operation of a park will be 52% higher than usual. And curiously, the only new park that works without incentives receives revenue from the market exclusively and from Natural Fenosa Gas. Natural Gas has 11 wind farms in Galicia with an installed power of 290 megawatts, and continues to advance in other processing. One of the problems experts pose is financial. Since the new payout from standards is reviewed every six years, this is still a risk that banks will take into account when financing new projects. Besides, electrical self-consumption is still an exception. From the sector, the big wind and power companies are accused of halting a process that is already common in Central Europe, with less power installed by apparatus than in Galicia, but more generators and more owners. Farms, industries, sports complexes, hotels, golf courses, are often equipped with a small electric generator and solar plates, without necessarily having channels to evacuate the network. In recent years, many companies in Galicia are applying for such authorizations, where it could be the start of an unstoppable process, favored by the premium crisis affecting large producers and aiming at the mentioned self-consumption. Only in self-consumption can there be a true energy democracy – and not a tariff change – that allows families to access energy and convert energy into a right and not a business. Nothing else. Thank you very much.

People of Galicia, at the initiative of Da Paula Prado del Río and seven other Members, on the adoption by the Greek Government of the measures necessary for the implementation of the guidelines provided for in the Plan for the Improvement of Public Procurement
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u212	This motion for a non-law amendment by the Socialist Parliamentary Group was tabled.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u213	To formulate the proposal you have the floor to Mrs. Prado del Río.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-05-26-DSPG110.u214	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, since 2009, when you entered the Government of Galicia, the People's Party, the contractual activity of public administrations has been given priority as a determining area of action in a twofold objective: on the one hand, to achieve a more efficient administration in the use of public resources, which is more open, understood and simplified from the point of view of citizens' and businesses' relations. From this priority, less than a year after the Government took possession, in January 2010, the Board approved Decree 3/2010 of 8 January, so it regulates electronic invoices, the use of electronic, computer and telematic means in the field of public procurement of the Glaga Autonomous Community Administration and entities of its independent public sector. This standard has started several orders at the same time: the Galician public procurement platform, the electronic bidding system and the electronic billing system. In this latter one we have to remember that it has been set as an objective, firstly, the payment of invoices in an average of 45 days, and today, according to the Ministry's data, we are at 17. I think it's an important figure in the number of days. Similarly, Decree 3/2010, of which I was talking, suggested an important advance, especially in what is the creation of Galicia's public procurement platform, and this is the second most visited website of Junta.es, – as you know – after the DOG page. This makes it a very useful and highly valued tool for the knowledge and follow-up of current contract procedures for the Peme, so that they know the possibility of business with the public sector and also easily access the bids of the Self-Government. From this platform, the entrepreneur can access the consultation of prior information announcements, current tenders, the most recent adjudications, the documentation of interest, both public opening dates and publications in other media, etc. Or of the contact points and ways to communicate with the attached recruitment bodies, which now exceed the two hundred. As corollary to this whole process of reform and modernization, Law 17/2010, of 17 December, of the organisation and operation of the General Administration and of the autonomous public sector of Galicia, as you know, has been approved in this Parliament. This law has made it possible to clarify, order and systematize the structure of the autonomous public sector, as a first step in introducing measures for rationalization and simplification of the German legal system and of the autonomous public sector. This regulation, as you know, is a major step forward in terms of innovation in regulating contracts in the Italian public sector, protecting by law principles and obligations aimed at raising the levels of efficiency, flexibility and transparency of these procedures and strengthening their strategic use. It was the need for a broad development of this law that led to the development of a plan of its own, which was approved at the June 12, 2014 Board of Trustees. This Public Procurement Improvement Plan 2014-2016 has had a number of shared objectives, which involve the Administration, the business fabric and the entire citizenship. The aim is to achieve greater efficiency and savings for the Administration in hiring services, works and supply, and a reduction in procedures for the contractor, ensuring payments in time and form, competition between offers and choice of best. Finally, an optimization in the use of public resources to continue doing more with less. Well, several measures of this plan have already given their fruit, since immediately all public-autonomic contracts have been applied to the simplified documentation regime. It also began to operate the restriction of direct adjudication procedures to half the limits that mark state legislation, demanding competition between three companies above these new limits. Therefore, Galicia became the first autonomous community with greater openness in our recruitment procedures. In addition, it increased – perhaps more in December last year – when the Board approved a model of clauses for negotiated procedures that would allow different companies to know, after the first stage, the essential characteristics of their competitors' bids, opening up the possibility of amending their initial proposal to introduce new improvements to their bids. In September 2014 two instruments published in the DOG were approved, as you know. On the one hand, the catalogue of services that may be self-sustaining, not to resort to external consulting, as was customary up to that time; the drafting of services that are centrally contracted, expanding a practice initiated in 2009 in contracts such as cleaning, maintenance, security, fuel, electricity. And also say that in February of this year Amtega has made the loyal platform available to the Town Councils to extend the electronic auction to the local administration as well. Well, now we have to move forward, in that plan for improved recruitment, in another aspect that is the object of this non-law proposition, which is the adoption of guides that allow us to apply the principles set out in that Rationalization Act. Thus, fulfilling the axis of the second plan, it would include the development of a public-public collaboration guide, or horizontal one, for the proper functioning of the self-supply mechanism. Here the guide must have as object to simply present the possibilities that collaborative techniques offer for better management of our autonomic public sector, and this self-supply model expressly excludes from the obligation of public tender, the two most typical assumptions of internal collaboration. On the one hand, management orders or domestic or in-house contracts; and alleged collaboration between public authorities to carry out a common service mission of them. On the 2nd axis of the plan, we talked about access and competition, it also provided for the development of a guide for open public procurement to small and medium-sized enterprises. In this sense, a number of general guidelines are introduced to be taken into account by the contracting bodies of the autonomic public sector, in order to ensure that tenders to be launched allow the competition of small and medium-sized enterprises, as well as of incipient companies. And in order to help the contracting bodies fulfil this legal mandate, it is necessary for the Board to push that guide for open public procurement to small and medium-sized enterprises, providing contracting bodies, practical guidance and examples to set up a contract design that facilitates the competition of small and medium-sized enterprises through different mechanisms, such as the requirement for batch division, the need for subcontracting, or even the adaptation of solvency and guarantee requirements to the possibilities of Peme. And finally, in execution of axis number 4, strategic and social use, we need to draw up an innovative public procurement guide and a guide for social and environmental clauses in public procurement. Indeed, Article 28 of Law 14/2013 of 27 December 2013 on the rationalization of the public sector marks as an incentive for innovative solutions to become legally a priority objective of Galicia's public procurement, and for this effective development of innovative public procurement, the role of the Administration should be strengthened as a driving force for business innovation. And for that, it must work, from the demand of the public manager, with the bidding, the public-innovative procurement contracts and from the supply side, that is, the companies competing in the tenders, helping them in their competitive participation and presenting innovative offers to these procurement procedures. Well, to give practical application to the mandates of the law of these assets I have just expressed, it is important to implement the plan of the Board that develops this law and to put in place that guide of social and environmental clauses in public procurement which includes measures, practical guidelines and examples to introduce forecasts in this area, both in administrative clauses and in technical contract requirements. That is why we urge the Council of Galicia to speed up its commitment to develop these instructions and thus give proper compliance with the legal obligations of improving public procurement contained in the Rationalization Act which now entered into force a year ago and which this group has supported, the difference between the rest of the House. Nothing more and thank you very much.
